(30 Oct; Worked; source: docs/research/learned-play-results.md#Champion ladder, first three tests (jobs 0020-0022; read 22:10 by manager run 22:08))
- **Lean loop without solver.** Dropping the exact solver and its database from training cost little if anything on tight fights v2, so later experiments used this cheaper recipe. (30 Oct; Worked; source: docs/decisions.md#Lean recipe for experiments (owner away, 2026-10-30))
- **Low-HP start slice.** Starting a quarter of self-play games at very low HP did not help on the low-HP held-out fights it was aimed at. (30 Oct; Didn't work; source: docs/research/learned-play-results.md#Lean loop: no solve step, and a low-HP start slice (jobs 0023-0028; read 23:20 by manager run 22:08))
- **Round 0 vs round 1.** One self-play round after the first heuristic-trained net added a small but steady gain, mostly on elites, so self-play rounds stayed in the recipe. (31 Oct; Worked; source: docs/research/learned-play-results.md#Round 0 vs round 1 on tight2 (job 0041b; read 03:00 2026-10-31 by manager run 02:07))
- **Data scaling 5k to 40k.** Training the first net on more heuristic games helped, with 40k games per group beating 20k on both halves of tight fights v2 over three paired seeds. (31 Oct; Worked; source: docs/decisions.md#Data scaling: 80k next (owner away, 2026-10-31))
- **Distilling two-turn values.** Training the net to rank options the way two-turn search does made it slightly worse on elites over three seeds, so the idea was closed. (31 Oct; Didn't work; source: docs/decisions.md#Distillation closed; rates retested under depth (owner away, 2026-10-31))
- **Newer recipes vs champion.** All twelve nets from newer training recipes failed to beat the 10-29 champion on the ladder. (31 Oct; Didn't work; source: docs/research/learned-play-results.md#Ladder: no newer recipe beats the 10-29 champion (job 0057a6f, tests 0004-0015; read 17:05 2026-10-31 by manager run bb961751))
- **Data scaling to 80k.** Doubling again to 80k games per group showed no gain over 40k and both nets failed the ladder, so data scaling stopped there. (31 Oct to 1 Nov; Didn't work; source: docs/decisions.md#Data scaling: 80k shows no gain over 40k; no night recipe at 80k (owner away, 2026-11-01))
- **Monster lookahead inputs.** Showing the net each monster's likely next moves gained nothing beyond seed luck on held-out elites and bosses, so it stayed off. (31 Oct to 1 Nov; Didn't work; source: docs/decisions.md#Monster lookahead: no gain, stays off (owner away, 2026-11-01))

#### Unseen content

- **Warm start on Silent.** Fine-tuning Ironclad nets on Silent games showed the effects net starts ahead with no Silent games, but the edge over the names net vanished once fine-tuning began. (30 Oct; Didn't work; source: docs/decisions.md#Warm start as built (owner away, 2026-10-30))
- **Boss lead per boss.** On a new boss test set the nets kept about half their lead over the heuristic on the two unseen bosses with familiar cards, but lost clearly on The Guardian when the cards were unseen too. (30 Oct; Partly; source: docs/research/learned-play-results.md#Boss lead per boss (tight-bosses step 3; job 0015, read 18:05 by manager run 17:33))
- **Coverage pilot.** Training on more card kinds steadily raised the nets' play on held-out cards with falling returns per doubling, which moved generated content up the plan. (30 Oct to 31 Oct; Worked; source: docs/decisions.md#Coverage pilot read: generated content moves up (owner away, 2026-10-31))
- **Threshold and held-card rows.** The Guardian's Mode Shift and the slimes' split were shown to the net as one shared kind of input, but what the net learned from slimes did not carry over to The Guardian beyond noise. (30 Oct to 31 Oct; Didn't work; source: docs/decisions.md#Threshold rows: no transfer detected, layout stays (owner away, 2026-10-31))
- **Demon Form probe.** On held-out boss fights with Demon Form in the deck, the heuristic played it at 41% of first offers and the nets at 6.5%, showing the nets undervalue a power that pays off over later turns. (31 Oct; Built; source: docs/research/learned-play-results.md#Demon Form probe (job 0041c; read 03:00 2026-10-31 by manager run 02:07))
- **Generated-power bench first check.** Swapping official power cards for generated ones showed the net slightly ahead of the heuristic, but mostly because the heuristic values most generated powers at about zero, so the heuristic proved a weak yardstick there. (31 Oct; Partly; source: docs/research/learned-play-results.md#Generated powers, first check (registry-lite step 6; read 2026-10-31))
- **Rate features.** Inputs telling the net how much a power is worth per turn fixed its Demon Form habit but cost wins against The Guardian, so they stayed off. (31 Oct; Didn't work; source: docs/decisions.md#Rate features: Guardian cost real but smaller; test depth, not a feature fix (owner away, 2026-10-31))
- **Rate features under depth.** Rechecked under two-turn search, the per-turn power inputs still gained less on held-out bosses than the bar set in advance, so they stayed off. (31 Oct; Didn't work; source: docs/decisions.md#Rates under depth: rule fails, rates stay off (owner away, 2026-10-31))
- **Generated-power training arms.** Adding 16 or 128 generated powers to training did not help the net with unseen generated power cards on the held-out cells that held one. (31 Oct; Didn't work; source: docs/decisions.md#Generated-power arms: no transfer, content stays out of the recipe (owner away, 2026-10-31))
- **Names-only twin probe.** A net trained on card names with the same games lost to its effects twin on elites, more on held-out cards, so reading card effects earns its keep. (31 Oct; Worked; source: docs/research/learned-play-results.md#Ids twin (3 seeds), distillation (3 seeds), rate nets at two turns on the Guardian (read 20:20 2026-10-31 by manager run 44869c1c))
- **Simulated power effects feature.** Measuring each generated power's worth by playing a simple bot with and without it barely matched how much the card actually cost the nets, so no such feature was built. (31 Oct; Didn't work; source: docs/research/learned-play-results.md#Two first checks from the review (fork of manager run bb961751, 16:55 2026-10-31))
- **Depth repairs unseen powers.** A pre-registered check on fresh held-out boss fights confirmed that two-turn net search gains much more on decks holding an unseen power card than on decks without. (31 Oct to 1 Nov; Worked; source: docs/decisions.md#Depth repairs unseen powers: the check holds (owner away, 2026-11-01))

#### Tests and rulers

- **Tight fights v2.** The close-fight test grew to 1,226 calibrated deck-and-encounter cells at 20 seeds each, with error bars taken over cells because cells, not seeds, limited certainty. (29 Oct; Built; source: docs/decisions.md#Tight fights v2 as built (2026-10-29))
- **Seed check of night passes.** The four rounds that had passed on tight fights v2 were retrained at three training seeds, and none held up on held-out cards, so the night's passes were training luck. (29 Oct; Didn't work; source: docs/decisions.md#Seed check as built (owner away, 2026-10-29))
- **Champion ladder.** A new net now had to beat the current champion on fresh fights in a sequential paired test before it could be promoted. (30 Oct; Built; source: docs/specs/champion-ladder.md)
- **Enemy patterns investigation.** They asked whether nets need enemy identity and move patterns, and the investigation found the nets learn each familiar enemy anyway, with the real gaps on unseen content and stale intents at the end of a turn. (30 Oct; Partly; source: docs/research/monster-patterns-2026-10-30.md)
- **Oracle arm, 3-copy bench.** Training on the bench's own test powers barely showed on the one-copy generated bench, so the bench was rebuilt with three copies of the swapped card, which shows a little. (31 Oct to 1 Nov; Partly; source: docs/decisions.md#Oracle arm read: the 1-copy bench is blind, the 3-copy bench sees a little (owner away, 2026-11-01))

#### Tools and process

- **Manager role while away.** When they left on the evening of 10-29, Claude took over as manager with a state file, running experiments around the clock under standing grants. (29 Oct; Built; source: commit 0701b82c)
- **Job queue.** A file-based job queue started experiments unattended within the machine's thread and memory budget, so the lab could run day and night. (30 Oct; Built; source: docs/decisions.md#Job queue as built (owner away, 2026-10-30))
- **Big-picture reviews.** When the owner asked for faster progress, regular reviews by several analyst agents with skeptics began, and each one re-ranked the queue. (30 Oct; Built; source: docs/research/big-picture-2026-10-30.md)
- **Hang and memory fixes.** A review they asked for after a hung job and two out-of-memory deaths found net search scoring every leaf in one giant batch and jobs using all threads, and both were fixed. (31 Oct; Worked; source: docs/decisions.md#Hang and memory fixes (2026-10-31, owner present))

### Stretch 4: From single fights to an Act (1 Nov to 2 Nov)

74 things tried: Worked 21, Didn't work 12, Partly 8, Built 31, Dropped 2.

#### Engine

- **Amount expressions.** Damage that scales with something, as in Body Slam, Heavy Blade, Perfected Strike and X-cost Whirlwind, became one general rule written as data. (2 Nov; Built; source: docs/decisions.md#Amount expressions built (rules-engine spec 2; owner asked for the series, 2026-11-02))
- **Static modifiers.** Always-on effects like Strength and Dexterity became data-driven modifiers on damage and block, so new ones can be added without code. (2 Nov; Built; source: docs/decisions.md#Static modifiers spec confirmed and built (rules-engine spec 4; 2026-11-02))
- **Replacement hooks.** Effects that change damage as it lands, like Intangible or damage reflected back, got a general rule checked against the decompiled game. (2 Nov; Built; source: docs/decisions.md#Replacement hooks spec confirmed and built (rules-engine spec 5; 2026-11-02))
- **Picks inside a card.** Cards that ask the player to choose mid-play, like Survivor's discard or picking a card back from the discard pile, became possible. (2 Nov; Built; source: docs/decisions.md#Decision points spec confirmed and built (rules-engine spec 6; 2026-11-02))
- **Known top of draw pile.** The engine now tracks cards the player knows are on top of the draw pile, for effects that put a card back on top. (2 Nov; Built; source: docs/decisions.md#Known top spec confirmed and built (rules-engine spec 7; 2026-11-02))
- **Monster move patterns as data.** Each monster's move choice became a data table of conditions, so new or changed monsters can be written without code while existing fights stay identical. (2 Nov; Built; source: docs/decisions.md#Monster patterns spec confirmed and built (rules-engine spec 9; 2026-11-02))
- **Per-copy card variants.** A single copy of a card can now carry its own numbers, as Rampage's growing damage needs. (2 Nov; Built; source: docs/decisions.md#Per-copy overrides spec confirmed and built (rules-engine spec 8; 2026-11-02))
- **Content packs carry everything.** Content files loaded at runtime can now hold cards and powers of every type using every engine building block, so generated attacks and skills load too. (2 Nov; Built; source: docs/decisions.md#Content packs carry every atom (rules-engine spec 10 stage 1; 2026-11-02))

#### Search

- **Two-turn sample size check.** Four sampled draws turned out to be enough, and skipping two-turn search where one-turn's top choice looked safe would have lost too many useful overrides, so neither change was built. (1 Nov; Didn't work; source: docs/research/learned-play-results.md#Two-turn search: sample size and an offline gate (job 0057a8z8c; read 02:50 2026-11-01 by manager run 048566e9))
- **Selective two-turn search.** Searching the second turn only below the best few end-of-turn positions was faster but agreed with full search too rarely to pass its first check, so it was not merged. (1 Nov; Didn't work; source: docs/decisions.md#Selective two-turn search: fails its first check, not merged (owner away, 2026-11-01))
- **Run-HP at two turns.** Two-turn search did not save the HP the nets were losing, because the leaves still had no reason to care about HP in easy hallways. (1 Nov; Didn't work; source: docs/decisions.md#Run-HP at two turns fails; hallway HP loss is racing, not tempo (owner away, 2026-11-01))
- **HP price in search.** Adding a small value for every HP kept to each position search scores, with no retraining, saved about 2.75 HP per fight on the run-HP bench and the champion then lost less HP than the heuristic. (1 Nov; Worked; source: docs/decisions.md#HP bonus promoted (owner away, 2026-11-01))
- **HP price sweep on gauntlet.** No other size of the HP price beat the original one on the gauntlet's clear rate, and the largest price lost badly. (1 Nov; Didn't work; source: docs/decisions.md#Gauntlet first check and HP-price sweep: c = 0.01 stays (owner away, 2026-11-01))
- **Enemy-Strength price.** A search-time price on enemy Strength fixed the Gremlin Nob problem but lost clears on the gauntlet, mostly at bosses, so it stayed off. (1 Nov; Didn't work; source: docs/decisions.md#Enemy-Strength price fails at run level (owner away, 2026-11-01))
- **End-HP price.** The HP price was changed to count HP after the coming end-of-combat heal, which stopped two-turn search from wasting HP in easy hallways and saved HP per hallway on the run-HP bench. (1 Nov to 2 Nov; Worked; source: docs/decisions.md#End-HP bonus built and read (owner away, 2026-11-02))
- **Two-turn player on gauntlet.** The champion searched two turns deep with the end-HP price cleared the gauntlet more often than the same net one turn deep and than the heuristic, and became the product player. (2 Nov; Worked; source: docs/decisions.md#Two-turn @hpe is the product player (owner away, 2026-11-02))
- **Mixed depth for screens.** Playing the first three floors one turn deep and the rest two turns deep was level with full depth and saved time, so it became the default for screening reads. (2 Nov; Worked; source: docs/decisions.md#Mixed depth on the headline: screening default, headline reads keep full depth (owner away, 2026-11-02))
- **Line seed.** A two-turn player that sticks to the line its first search of the turn chose played level or slightly better than re-searching every decision, which opened the way to cheaper depth. (2 Nov; Worked; source: docs/decisions.md#Line seed passes: stage 2 (export the principal line) is justified after the landing (owner away, 2026-11-02))

#### Training the net

- **Burst net becomes champion.** The burst net passed the ladder against the 10-29 champion while a fine-tune without the generated cards did not, so it was promoted, though the gauntlet later showed it was not better at run level. (1 Nov; Worked; source: docs/decisions.md#Burst8 s1 promoted to champion (owner away, 2026-11-01))
- **Burst ratchet and more cards.** A second burst on top of the first, or bursts with 16 or 32 generated cards, were level with the champion, so burst work for the ladder closed. (1 Nov; Didn't work; source: docs/decisions.md#Bursts: ratchet and more cards closed (owner away, 2026-11-01))
- **HP weight in labels.** Weighting HP lost more heavily in training labels saved about 2.4 HP per fight on the run-HP bench with no extra deaths, and a burst trained this way became champion. (1 Nov; Worked; source: docs/decisions.md#Burst8hp2 s1 promoted; promotion now also checks run-HP (owner away, 2026-11-01))
- **Unblocked-incoming feature.** An input for damage about to land unblocked was planned and replaced within the hour by the start-HP column, after a probe found the real gap. (1 Nov; Dropped; source: docs/decisions.md#Start-HP column replaces `unblocked_incoming` (owner away, 2026-11-01))
- **Start-HP column.** Giving the net the HP the fight started at let it price HP and passed its rule, but it bought nothing the search-time HP price did not already give. (1 Nov; Partly; source: docs/decisions.md#Start-HP column passes but the bonus stays (owner away, 2026-11-01))
- **Randomized enemy HP idea.** They asked whether random enemy HP could make solvable endgames to learn from, and an investigation said no: the solvable ones are almost all safe wins. (1 Nov; Dropped; source: docs/research/random-enemy-hp-2026-11-01.md)
- **Survival score.** Their design had the net predict HP lost from now and search value survival at a fixed price, but it saved less HP than the simple search-time price and cost some wins. (1 Nov to 3 Nov; Didn't work; the owner's call; source: docs/decisions.md#Survival score: HP lost from now, survival at a fixed price (owner present, 2026-11-01))
- **Back to the 10-29 net.** The older 10-29 net beat the later ladder champion on the gauntlet and in a pre-registered confirmation, so it became the product net and training since 10-29 was shown not to reach run level. (2 Nov; Worked; source: docs/decisions.md#Product net: the 10-29 net (owner away, 2026-11-02))
- **Training rule.** They agreed that training only runs when it has a run-level reason, after the review found training took a large share of machine time while each round beyond the first gave almost nothing. (2 Nov; Worked; the owner's call; source: docs/decisions.md#Training: when it runs and when it stops (owner, 2026-11-02))
- **New row kinds opt-in.** The new kinds of input rows from the series are written only when a net asks for them, because by default they nearly doubled the rows per position. (2 Nov; Built; source: docs/decisions.md#Series row kinds are opt-in (2026-11-02, confirmed by the owner))
- **Two-turn self-play round.** One training round with the two-turn product as teacher was kept as a single targeted try, and both arms failed the gauntlet protocol, closing that family of training. (2 Nov to 3 Nov; Didn't work; the owner's call; source: docs/decisions.md#Two-turn self-play round: kept, small and targeted (owner, 2026-11-02))
- **Solver step at run level.** Nets trained without the exact solver were level with the 10-29 recipe on the gauntlet, and a hint of a gap on the headline setting did not show over three seeds. (2 Nov to 3 Nov; Didn't work; source: docs/research/learned-play-results.md#Seed twins of the solve step on the headline: not shown on three seeds (headline re-rank's cheap check; 0063; read 00:20 2026-11-03, manager caf615f4))

#### Run choices

- **Prior art on StS bots.** A scan of existing Slay the Spire bots and run-level work pointed to tuning the HP price on Act clears rather than HP per fight. (1 Nov; Built; source: docs/research/sts-bots-and-runs.md)
- **Card rewards on the gauntlet.** After each won fight the AI picks one of three cards, and from the starter deck a chooser that simulates each card in fights of the next floor cleared far more runs than random picks, while skipping every reward never cleared. (2 Nov; Worked; source: docs/decisions.md#Card rewards are a goal-2 lever (owner away, 2026-11-02))
- **Sim-boss card chooser.** Letting the card chooser also simulate each option against the boss made it draft scaling cards like Spot Weakness and Inflame and cut boss deaths, and this became the headline setting. (2 Nov; Worked; source: docs/decisions.md#Sim-boss passes: new headline setting (owner away, 2026-11-02))
- **HP price by floor.** Fitting how much an HP point is worth at each gauntlet floor found the price roughly flat along the floors. (2 Nov; Built; source: docs/research/learned-play-results.md#Run value: what an HP is worth by floor (Next Q4; offline, 2026-11-02))
- **Rest-site chooser.** Choosing between healing and upgrading at the rest before the boss by simulating boss fights let lucky upgrades beat healing too often, and it failed at the first look. (2 Nov; Didn't work; source: docs/decisions.md#Rest-site chooser closed (owner away, 2026-11-02))
- **Card removal.** Removing a Strike after the first elite was worth about 1.8 HP at the end of the run on the headline setting, but a chooser that picked which card to remove did worse than always removing a Strike. (2 Nov; Partly; source: docs/decisions.md#Card removal read: a removal is worth ~1.8 HP, the chooser is closed (owner away, 2026-11-02))
- **Elite skip.** Skipping every elite showed path choice is worth a lot on this gauntlet, while the plain rule of skipping an elite below 40 HP captured only a small part of it and was not adopted. (2 Nov; Partly; source: docs/decisions.md#Elite-skip read: path choice is real, the headline keeps no path rule (owner away, 2026-11-02))
- **Run value along HP.** The chance of beating the boss rises steeply with HP on arrival, and the check for using that curve as the run's price passed on floors six to eight and failed on floor five. (2 Nov; Partly; the owner's call; source: docs/decisions.md#Run value along HP: the plan (owner, 2026-11-02))
- **Boss replays.** Replaying each deck's boss fight at different entry HP showed the HP-to-win curve is causal and differs per boss, with The Guardian a cliff and Hexaghost a slow climb. (2 Nov; Worked; source: docs/decisions.md#Boss replays: the boss curve is causal; the run value is per boss (owner away, 2026-11-02))
- **Run-value card chooser.** Scoring card picks by the fitted run value instead of HP after the next fight cleared fewer runs than the plain sim chooser, so card picks kept sim-boss. (2 Nov; Didn't work; source: docs/decisions.md#Run-value chooser: fails, use (b) closes for card picks (owner away, 2026-11-02))
- **Crossed chooser arms.** Swapping the card chooser and the fighter between the net and the heuristic showed the chooser carries more of the net's lead than the fighting does, nearly all of it on held-out cards. (2 Nov; Built; source: docs/decisions.md#Crossed chooser arms read: the chooser is the larger one-turn lever (owner away, 2026-11-02))

#### Unseen content

- **Adaptation burst.** A ten-minute fine-tune on games holding eight generated power cards taught the net those cards, narrowly under its bar, and surprisingly also made it beat the champion on official cards. (1 Nov; Partly; source: docs/research/learned-play-results.md#Burst control: the gain comes from the generated powers (jobs 0059d1-d6; read 05:20 2026-11-01 by manager run 68e81bce))
- **Depth on generated powers.** Two-turn search helped with generated powers about as much as with official cards, so depth fixes the net's errors broadly rather than new mechanics in particular. (1 Nov; Didn't work; source: docs/decisions.md#Depth on generated powers: not content-specific (owner away, 2026-11-01))
- **Novelty cost past the horizon.** Unseen powers whose payoff lands after the end of the searched turn were the ones the net mis-priced, and a pre-registered replication on fresh fights confirmed that depth repairs them. (1 Nov to 2 Nov; Worked; source: docs/research/learned-play-results.md#Horizon replication passes (0059l7r3a9; read 03:05 2026-11-02, manager c3513fb5))
- **Silent zero-shot under depth.** On Silent decks never trained on, the champion was level with the heuristic one turn deep and ahead two turns deep, with Poison decks gaining most from depth. (2 Nov; Partly; source: docs/research/learned-play-results.md#Silent zero-shot number and depth on Silent (0059l7r3a3-a6; read 00:20 2026-11-02, manager c3513fb5))
- **Goal-1 paired modifiers.** They agreed that new content for goal 1 would be official cards and monsters with a boon and a bane paired, in the style of the Chimera Cards mod, measured one modifier at a time. (2 Nov; Built; the owner's call; source: docs/decisions.md#Goal-1 yardstick: paired modifiers, measured per modifier (owner, 2026-11-02))
- **Mutated monsters stage A.** Changing elites' and bosses' HP or Strength cost the net little beyond the heuristic, an unseen Sharp Hide on Nob and Sentries cost it a lot one turn deep, and two-turn search removed that cost. (2 Nov; Partly; source: docs/decisions.md#Mutated monsters stage A, two-turn read: horizon, not a training gap; no burst on mutated monsters (owner away, 2026-11-02))
- **Effects vs names under depth.** On the generated-power bench the effects net's edge over the names net was large one turn deep, but two-turn search took over most of it. (2 Nov; Partly; source: docs/decisions.md#Q6 two turns: search takes over most of the encoding's edge on unseen cards (owner away, 2026-11-02))
- **Effects edge at run level.** On the gauntlet the names net lost HP and clears to its effects twin trained on the same games, almost all of it on held-out cards. (2 Nov; Worked; source: docs/research/learned-play-results.md#Effects vs ids, the solve step and threshold rows at run level (approach review cheap reads 2, 3, 5; b39abb; read 16:20 2026-11-02, manager 3a78b41d))

#### Tests and rulers

- **Ladder v2 and run-HP bench.** The ladder got cached results and fixed-size reads, plus a bench that measures HP lost per fight at run-like starting HP. (1 Nov; Built; source: docs/decisions.md#Ladder v2 spec confirmed (owner away, 2026-11-01))
- **Run-HP first check.** At run-like starting HP every net won as often as the heuristic but lost about twice its HP, mostly by dragging multi-monster hallways out. (1 Nov; Built; source: docs/research/learned-play-results.md#Run-HP first check (jobs 0059c1-c3; read 04:55 2026-11-01 by manager run 68e81bce))
- **Tempo probe.** The champion turned out to win hallways slightly faster than the heuristic while taking more damage per turn, so it was under-blocking, not stalling. (1 Nov; Built; source: docs/decisions.md#Run-HP at two turns fails; hallway HP loss is racing, not tempo (owner away, 2026-11-01))
- **Act 1 gauntlet.** A new yardstick chains 11 fights like Act 1, from easy hallways to elites and a boss, with HP carried over, Burning Blood's heal and a rest before the boss, and counts how often the run clears. (1 Nov; Built; source: docs/decisions.md#Gauntlet spec confirmed (owner away, 2026-11-01))
- **Gremlin Nob probe.** Gauntlet runs whose first elite was Gremlin Nob showed the HP price made the net play more Skills into Nob's Enrage, and a heavy price lost noticeably more Nob fights. (1 Nov; Built; source: docs/research/learned-play-results.md#Act-1 gauntlet: first check and HP-price sweep (0059l4a-l4f; read 16:30 2026-11-01, manager))
- **Ladder GSPRT replay.** Replaying every ladder test under a different sequential statistic changed no verdict, so the ladder's test was kept. (1 Nov; Built; source: docs/research/learned-play-results.md#Ladder replay under a trinomial GSPRT (manager fork, 16:57 2026-11-01))
- **Gauntlet protocol and early stop.** Gauntlet comparisons became a sequential paired test on HP left at the end of the run, which stops as soon as the verdict is clear and saved two to three times the CPU per read. (2 Nov; Built; source: docs/decisions.md#Gauntlet early stop built (owner away, 2026-11-02))
- **Rollout ceiling probe.** Replaying each failed run's fatal fight with many play-outs per move rarely saved it, so losses were decided earlier by HP spent on the way and by the deck. (2 Nov; Built; source: docs/research/learned-play-results.md#Rollout ceiling probe (0059l7r3b27-b29b; read 03:05 2026-11-02, manager c3513fb5))
- **Heuristic two-turn anchor.** The heuristic searched two turns deep gained about as much from depth as the net adds at equal depth, with the net's edge mostly HP carried to the boss. (2 Nov; Built; source: docs/research/learned-play-results.md#Heuristic two-turn anchor, first half; early stop checked (0059l7r3b3, b355, b361; read 04:20 2026-11-02, manager 61d75983))
- **Run-level seed noise.** Nets trained with different seeds of one recipe played the gauntlet almost alike, so a recipe's run-level result can be trusted from a few seeds. (2 Nov; Built; source: docs/research/learned-play-results.md#Run-level seed noise (twins b37d-f, 2026-11-02))
- **Bias audit.** Adding Strength or Poison to an enemy and comparing the net's score with play-outs showed the net prices enemy Strength at about a quarter of its real cost and Poison as a small fixed bonus. (2 Nov; Built; source: docs/research/learned-play-results.md#Bias audit, slice 1: the leaf under-prices enemy Strength ~4x and Poison 2-6x (0059l7r3b37; read 05:45 2026-11-02, manager 61d75983))
- **Headline number.** The 10-29 net searched two turns deep with the end-HP price cleared 0.804 of 500 Act 1 gauntlet runs from the starter deck with simulated card rewards, well ahead of one-turn play and the heuristic. (2 Nov; Worked; source: docs/decisions.md#Headline number: two-turn product on starter + rewards + sim-boss clears 0.804 (owner away, 2026-11-02))
- **Headline re-rank of nets.** With card rewards in the loop every other standing net trailed the 10-29 net, so the headline setting became the final ruler and the tight-fight ladder stopped deciding the product. (2 Nov; Worked; source: docs/decisions.md#Headline re-rank: the 10-29 net is the product net, the headline setting is the final ruler (owner away, 2026-11-02))

#### Tools and process

- **Review stop list.** A review found depth the only lever that repeatedly beat noise, so training experiments judged only on one-turn held-out tight fights were stopped. (1 Nov; Worked; source: docs/decisions.md#Review 2026-11-01: stop list adopted (owner away, 2026-11-01))
- **Fast search hash tables.** A faster hash for search's position tables sped up two-turn search with exactly the same results. (1 Nov; Worked; source: docs/decisions.md#Fast search tables built (owner away, 2026-11-01))
- **Net leaf cache.** The Rust net now computes each distinct card and power row once per call, making net-scored search faster with bit-identical scores. (1 Nov; Worked; source: docs/decisions.md#Net leaf cache built (owner away, 2026-11-01))
- **Heuristic rate cache.** Caching the heuristic's walk over each monster's moves made two-turn heuristic search about five times faster with identical results. (1 Nov; Worked; source: docs/decisions.md#Heuristic rate cache built (owner away, 2026-11-01))
- **Gauntlet thread check.** A one-turn gauntlet job barely ran faster on eight threads than on three, so throughput came from running two jobs at once instead. (2 Nov; Built; source: docs/research/learned-play-results.md#One-turn gauntlet: 8 threads barely beat 3 (Q7; b2z1/b2z2, 2026-11-02))
- **Approach review.** They asked for a high-level review of every approach, and 99 read-only agents with skeptics found every recent gain came from search or run decisions rather than training, and re-ranked the plan. (2 Nov; Built; source: docs/research/approach-review-2026-11-02.md)
- **Search memo stores once.** Search stopped copying whole positions in and out of its tables, a speed change with identical results that the series profile pointed at. (2 Nov; Worked; source: docs/decisions.md#Search memo stores each state once (2026-11-02))
- **Series reviews and fixes.** Reviewers with skeptics checked the rules-engine branch, and the fixes that touched the live training pipeline were made before it landed. (2 Nov; Built; source: docs/decisions.md#Series review fixes: the cut (2026-11-02, manager's call, owner present))
- **Lanes job runner.** The job queue gained lanes with shares of the machine, memory reservations and jobs pinned to a snapshot of their code, so a rebuild never disturbs a running job. (2 Nov; Built; source: docs/decisions.md#Lanes runner as built (owner present, 2026-11-02))
- **kb claims store.** A store of sourced facts about the project was turned on and seeded, so status and decisions could be looked up instead of re-derived. (2 Nov; Built; source: docs/decisions.md#kb adoption spec confirmed (owner present, 2026-11-02))
- **Product registry.** The product player now lives in one tracked file that only a promotion script changes. (2 Nov; Built; source: docs/decisions.md#Product registry: configs/product.json, promoted only by product.py (owner present, 2026-11-02))
- **Spec status line.** Every spec now states its status on line three in a fixed word list, checked by a script, so specs in flight can be listed. (2 Nov; Built; source: docs/decisions.md#Spec status is line 3 of each spec (owner present, 2026-11-02))

### Stretch 5: The AI roles take over (3 Nov to 5 Nov)

35 things tried: Worked 10, Didn't work 6, Partly 7, Built 11, Dropped 1.

#### Engine

- **Rules-engine upgrade landed.** The big rules-engine upgrade branch landed on main with every recorded test game replaying exactly as before, and new nets got a wider input layout. (3 Nov; Built; source: docs/decisions.md#engine-next landed (owner's OK of 2026-11-02; landed 00:20 2026-11-03, owner away))
- **Act 1 as content files.** The owner decided the real Act 1 would enter the engine as data files read at runtime rather than as Rust code, so any card that cannot be written as data points to a missing building block. (4 Nov; Built; the owner's call; source: docs/decisions.md#Runtime registry first: Act 1 enters as content files (owner, 2026-11-04))
- **Lane runtime-registry.** Moved all official content (128 cards, 30 powers, 18 monsters) out of Rust code into content files read at start, with every recorded game still identical. (4 Nov to 6 Nov; Worked; rated likely beforehand)

#### Act 1 content

- **Wiki as rules source.** With no copy of the game installed, the owner first ruled that card and monster numbers come from the wiki and the sts_lightspeed simulator, a rule replaced on 11-10 by the game's own code. (4 Nov; Dropped; the owner's call; source: docs/decisions.md#No StS1 install; content parity from the wiki and sts_lightspeed (owner, 2026-11-04))

#### Search

- **Lane cheap-depth.** Tried cheaper ways to search two turns deep. Scoring a turn's end after a few sampled draws failed its puzzle test, but line step (play the planned line until the next random event) made the same decisions faster and became how the product plays. (3 Nov; Worked; rated even beforehand; 8 CPU-hours)
- **Lane depth-headroom.** Asked whether searching deeper than two turns would help. Rollouts to the end of the fight and a three-turn search both gave nothing on the second elite, so the depth family was closed. (3 Nov to 4 Nov; Didn't work; rated long shot beforehand; 8.8 CPU-hours)

#### Training the net

- **Lane two-turn-round.** Fine-tuned the net on 3,000 games played by the stronger two-turn player. Both training seeds played 1.0 to 1.4 HP worse per run one turn deep on the fixed-deck test, and training on game outcomes was closed on this laptop. (3 Nov; Didn't work; rated long shot beforehand; 20 CPU-hours)
- **Lane burst-official.** Checked whether a net fine-tuned on new monster mechanics also plays the official headline better. It gained on decks with held-out cards, but not enough overall. (5 Nov to 6 Nov; Didn't work; rated even beforehand; 4.6 CPU-hours)

#### Run choices

- **Lane run-decisions.** Three run-level probes: rest or smith before the boss, skipping elites, and an HP rule for elites. None reached the 1.5 HP bar; skipping elites gained clears only because elites drop no relic in the test. (3 Nov to 4 Nov; Didn't work; rated long shot beforehand; 6.5 CPU-hours)

#### Unseen content

- **Navigator's first defaults.** On its first run the navigator settled the owner's open questions with the easiest-to-undo choice, starting with goal 1 meaning new combinations of building blocks the engine already has. (3 Nov; Built; source: docs/decisions.md#Navigator's first defaults (on the owner's behalf, 2026-11-03))
- **Lane goal1-yardstick.** Built the owner's test for unseen content: official monsters given one change each (more Strength, more HP, Sharp Hide 2, Ritual 2 and others), each measured against the hand-written player. Two turns deep the product paid almost nothing extra per change. (3 Nov to 5 Nov; Built; rated even beforehand; 90.1 CPU-hours)
- **Lane adaptation-curve.** Measured how many practice games a net needs for a new monster mechanic. About 100 games already cut Sharp Hide 2's cost without hurting old play; the lane had underrated its chances and overestimated its CPU. (4 Nov; Worked; rated even beforehand; 1.8 CPU-hours)
- **Lane adaptation.** Checked whether practice on Sharp Hide carries over. It did, to Thorns and to Weak-when-hit; practice did nothing for Ritual 2, whose payoff lies past the search's horizon. (4 Nov to 5 Nov; Partly; rated even beforehand; 3.4 CPU-hours)
- **Lane difficulty-shift.** Separated a harder monster from a new mechanic. More Strength cost the net nothing on 325 boss starts (an earlier boss cost turned out to be seed luck), and practice on bigger numbers did not help with Sharp Hide or Thorns. (5 Nov; Partly; rated likely beforehand; 6.1 CPU-hours)
- **Lane boss-cost-two-turn.** Looked for a boss cost of the monster changes two turns deep. Nothing beyond noise, at twice the CPU estimate. (5 Nov; Didn't work; rated long shot beforehand; 17.9 CPU-hours)
- **Lane adapted-two-turn.** Played a net that had practised against Sharp Hide two turns deep. It won more of those elite fights than the product, at a small cost on the normal elites. (5 Nov; Worked; rated even beforehand; 24 CPU-hours)
- **Lane unseen-boss-burst.** Gave nets 400 and 1,600 practice games against The Guardian and Hexaghost. The Guardian improved as after any practice; Hexaghost not at all. (5 Nov; Didn't work; rated even beforehand; 1.9 CPU-hours)
- **Lane sequential-bursts.** Taught two new mechanics one after the other. The second lesson kept most or all of the first. (5 Nov; Worked; rated even beforehand; 1.2 CPU-hours)
- **Lane yardstick-cards.** The unseen-card test. Of ten real cards the nets never trained on, only Demon Form cost them; two training recipes raised Demon Form decks, but only for its first turn of Strength, and neither beat an earlier fine-tune. (5 Nov to 7 Nov; Partly; rated even beforehand; 47.4 CPU-hours)

#### Tests and rulers

- **Big-picture reviews, 3-12 Nov.** Reviews by six analysts with skeptic checks ran on six days, and by 11-12 they found the product had not improved since 11-03 although about 865 thread-hours of runs had gone into it. (3 Nov to 12 Nov; Partly; source: docs/research/big-picture-2026-11-12.md#Diagnosis)
- **Threads per headline read.** A two-turn headline read on 8 threads finished in 0.6 of the time of 3 threads but used 1.6 times the CPU per run, so 3 threads stayed the default and 8 is used only for a read that runs alone. (4 Nov; Partly; source: docs/decisions.md#Thread count for headline reads: 3 threads stays the default; 8 only for a read alone on the machine (on the owner's behalf, 2026-11-04))
- **Lane cheaper-reads.** Made headline reads cheaper by replaying the same card picks for both players. One turn deep that cut the noise 2.4 to 4.2 times and became a screening tool; two turns deep it fell short of its bar. (4 Nov; Partly; rated likely beforehand; 11.4 CPU-hours)
- **Boss cost re-read.** Re-reading the mutated-monster table showed a plain +2 Strength boss cost the net one turn deep as much as Ritual 2 did, and a later look found those boss costs were mostly one lucky draw of the unmodified fights. (5 Nov; Partly; source: docs/decisions.md#Stage A re-read: the boss cost follows shifted numbers, not growth, and how a novelty cost is read (on the owner's behalf, 2026-11-05))
- **Seed spread on bosses.** Short boss replays measured how much two training seeds of the same net differ on The Guardian, Hexaghost and Slime Boss, about 0.01 to 0.02 wins, which later bars on boss fights were set against. (5 Nov; Worked; source: docs/decisions.md#Boss-replay probes: the seed spread on unseen bosses, the ensemble, and unseen-boss-burst's two-turn rule (on the owner's behalf, 2026-11-05))
- **Compare one turn deep.** The owner ruled that every comparison plays one turn deep, because changes kept their direction two turns deep at about a third of the size while a two-turn game costs about 100 times as much, and the two-turn product stays the reported score. (5 Nov; Worked; the owner's call; source: docs/decisions.md#Comparisons play one turn deep; two-turn search is the product's augment (owner, 2026-11-05))
- **Choosing the kind of read.** The owner set a guide for comparisons: each side picks its own cards when the question is the whole player, and both replay the control's card picks when the question is only fighting. (5 Nov; Built; the owner's call; source: docs/decisions.md#Choosing a read by its question: own picks, matched, or crossed (owner, 2026-11-05))
- **Held-out cards read.** Instead of training new nets overnight as the owner proposed, the navigator re-read existing files and found that cards a net never trained on cost it about half its edge over the hand-written player on cell-gauntlet runs. (5 Nov; Worked; source: docs/decisions.md#The owner's filler proposal placed: read the held-out half of existing nets, not full-size ids twins; unseen cards cost the net half its edge (on the owner's behalf, 2026-11-05))

#### Tools and process

- **Three AI roles take over.** The owner handed day-to-day running to three AI roles: a steward that checks the machine's health, a navigator that picks the work, and lane workers that each run one short project with a CPU-hour cap and an end date. (3 Nov; Built; the owner's call; source: docs/decisions.md#Operating model confirmed: steward, navigator, project lanes (owner, 2026-11-03))
- **Steward health checks.** A health-check script was written that turns a blocker red (a dead job runner, an unpushed main, a failed test run) and wakes the right role once per event. (3 Nov; Built; source: da670840)
- **Role launcher.** A launcher loop was built that starts the navigator or a lane worker as a background Claude session when there is work, and stops any session that runs over its time. (3 Nov; Built; the owner's call; source: docs/decisions.md#Role launcher as built (owner, 2026-11-03))
- **Read-only dashboard.** A local read-only web page was built that shows at a glance the health light, running sessions and jobs, and the open lanes, with each role session readable as a conversation. (3 Nov to 5 Nov; Built; the owner's call; source: docs/decisions.md#Dashboard as built (owner, 2026-11-03))
- **Jobs pinned to snapshots.** Queued jobs were made to run from a frozen copy of the commit that queued them, also when queued from a lane's own folder, so rebuilding the main engine never changes a job already in the queue. (4 Nov; Built; source: c4537b65)
- **Standing filler job.** So the CPU would not sit idle while lanes wrote specs, a standing background job (the one-turn control on the held-out half of the headline) was named to fill the gaps. (4 Nov; Built; source: docs/decisions.md#Standing filler successor and the review's timing (on the owner's behalf, 2026-11-04))
- **Lane test-full-memory.** Made the full test suite fit beside the live work. Run file by file it peaked at 1.8 GB instead of 9.8 GB. (5 Nov to 7 Nov; Worked; rated likely beforehand; 23.4 CPU-hours)

### Stretch 6: Real Act 1 goes in (6 Nov to 9 Nov)

65 things tried: Worked 24, Didn't work 12, Partly 11, Built 17, Dropped 1.

#### Engine

- **Lane sparse-state.** Shrank the game state from 1,292 to 744 bytes. Search ran 17.6% faster on one thread, with identical games. (6 Nov; Worked; rated likely beforehand; 2 CPU-hours)

#### Act 1 content

- **Act 1 inventory.** An inventory of the real Act 1 against the engine found 7 of the 25 Act 1 monsters and 40 of the 75 Ironclad cards missing, and ranked the missing building blocks by how many cards each unblocks. (6 Nov; Built; source: docs/research/act1-inventory.md#Counts)
- **First monsters and cards as data.** Four missing monsters (with the Gremlin Wizard's first Blast on turn 3, as the wiki says) and sixteen Ironclad cards that needed no new engine pieces were written as content files. (6 Nov; Built; source: docs/research/act1-inventory.md#Step 3: the sixteen writable Ironclad cards as pack data)
- **Block C1 exhaust cards.** Block C1 added six cards that exhaust other cards (True Grit, Burning Pact, Second Wind, Sever Soul, Fiend Fire, Exhume), with base True Grit's random exhaust as a chance event. (6 Nov; Built; source: docs/decisions.md#Exhaust-cards spec (C1): exclusion by mechanic, count before exhaust, True Grit as chance (on the owner's behalf, 2026-11-06))
- **Block M5 monster powers.** Block M5 let monsters written as data start a fight with their own powers, which loaded the Mad Gremlin's Angry. (6 Nov; Built; source: docs/decisions.md#Monster-pack-powers spec (M5): start powers only, bound after the deck's (on the owner's behalf, 2026-11-06))
- **Lane act1-content.** Added real Act 1 content as data: all 7 missing monsters and 27 cards, with 13 cards blocked by missing building blocks and relics not started. From 9 November the owner carried Act 1 content on in their own sessions. (6 Nov to 9 Nov; Partly; rated likely beforehand; 36.8 CPU-hours)
- **Block C2 exhaust triggers.** Block C2 added Dark Embrace, Feel No Pain and Sentinel, with their triggers firing after the card in play as the game orders them. (7 Nov; Built; source: docs/decisions.md#Exhaust-triggers spec (C2): fire after the card, owed turn-end draws (on the owner's behalf, 2026-11-07))
- **Block S1 more slots.** Block S1 raised how many data cards and powers one fight can hold to 32 and 24, so decks made mostly of new cards could be played. (7 Nov; Built; source: docs/decisions.md#act1-content: next block S1, more pack cards and powers per fight (on the owner's behalf, 2026-11-07))
- **Block C4 Entrench, Limit Break.** Block C4 added block and power amounts read from the current state, which wrote Entrench and Limit Break. (7 Nov; Built; source: docs/decisions.md#act1-content C4 build: ApplyOf takes official powers only (on the owner's behalf, 2026-11-07))
- **Block G1 new cards offered.** Block G1 put the new Ironclad cards into the gauntlet's card rewards, and over 500 headline runs every player's clears dropped (the product from 0.834 to 0.684) while their order stayed the same. (7 Nov; Worked; source: docs/research/act1-inventory.md#G1 re-rank read (0463, 0464, 0465))
- **Block E1 encounters as data.** Block E1 made encounters themselves data, so fights like the Gremlin Gang and Exordium Thugs are described in content files. (8 Nov; Built; source: docs/specs/encounters-as-data.md)
- **Blocks M6, M7 last monsters.** Blocks M6 and M7 added the Shield Gremlin (block on a random ally) and the Red Slaver (Entangle), the last of the 25 Act 1 monsters. (8 Nov; Built; source: docs/decisions.md#Blocks M6 and M7: ally block at MonsterRolls; Entangled as a pack power (on the owner's behalf, 2026-11-08))
- **Block G2 new encounters.** Block G2 put the new encounters into the gauntlet's hard floors, and on headline runs the new monsters cost the 10-29 net no more than they cost the hand-written player. (8 Nov; Worked; source: docs/research/act1-inventory.md#Block G2: pack encounters in the gauntlet's hard floors)
- **Thugs parity check.** The real Exordium Thugs cost the 10-29 net about 3.3 HP more than the engine's old version over 3,504 paired fights, because the real encounter can hold a Slaver, and no bug was found. (9 Nov; Worked; source: docs/research/act1-inventory.md#Thugs parity replay (0676))
- **Block C3 draw triggers.** Block C3 added Evolve and Fire Breathing, firing once per Status card drawn. (9 Nov; Built; source: docs/decisions.md#Draw triggers: once per Status card (on the owner's behalf, 2026-11-09))
- **Block C5 cost changes.** Block C5 added cards whose cost changes in a fight, Blood for Blood and Corruption. (9 Nov; Built; source: docs/decisions.md#C5 cost changes: one HP-loss counter and two power fields (on the owner's behalf, 2026-11-09))
- **Card sets as lists.** The card lists used for training and testing moved out of the card definitions into one named-list file, so adding new cards never changes an existing list. (9 Nov; Built; source: docs/decisions.md#Card sets as built (on the owner's behalf, 2026-11-09))
- **Relic and potion inventory.** An inventory listed the 141 relics and 33 potions an Act 1 run can meet, none of them in the engine yet, with the building blocks each one needs. (9 Nov; Built; source: docs/research/act1-relics-potions.md#Counts)
- **Owner works on Act 1 directly.** The owner made finishing Act 1 a standing goal, then moved Act 1 content into their own sessions after a lane needed about 3.5 hours of spec and review for two cards. (9 Nov to 12 Nov; Worked; the owner's call; source: docs/decisions.md#act1-content parked; the owner leads Act 1 content (owner, 2026-11-09))

#### Search

- **Lane turn-start-leaf.** Scored the end of a turn after the player's start-of-turn effects, such as Demon Form's Strength. Demon Form decks gained about 7.5 HP per run on the fixed-deck test with no training; it joined the product on 13 November. (6 Nov to 7 Nov; Worked; rated even beforehand; 0.7 CPU-hours)
- **Turn-start price everywhere.** Pricing turn-start powers in every search, card-pick trial fights included, read level on the headline one turn deep because the card chooser then took Demon Form on 0.49 of offers instead of 0.17 and lost HP at the elites. (7 Nov; Didn't work; source: docs/decisions.md#After the turn-start leaf on the one-turn headline: the one-turn controls stay plain; the chooser's horizon overpicks Demon Form (on the owner's behalf, 2026-11-07))
- **Lane hands-where-small.** Sampled more possible next hands where the search tree is small. A fraction of an HP per elite fight, wins unmoved. (7 Nov; Didn't work; rated long shot beforehand; 4.3 CPU-hours)
- **Lane leaf-blind-numbers.** Looked for numbers the net misreads. It priced leftover block with the wrong sign; the fix changed few fights at first, and joined the product on 13 November under the owner's 'level is a pass' rule. (7 Nov; Partly; rated even beforehand; 0.55 CPU-hours)
- **Lane projected-leaf.** Projected Strength and Dexterity gains a few turns ahead at the end of the search. HP improved only in fights already won. (7 Nov; Didn't work; rated long shot beforehand; 3.4 CPU-hours)
- **Strength price held-out read.** A search price for enemy Strength that counts the hits left in a fight broke none of 16 held-out made-up mechanics and saved about 0.4 HP per elite fight on average, mostly on Gremlin Nob. (8 Nov; Worked; source: docs/decisions.md#After shl-held-out: the Strength price's trade stays on one cell; its one-turn headline read queued (on the owner's behalf, 2026-11-08))
- **Strength price on headline.** On the headline one turn deep the same Strength price read level (+0.95 HP over 500 runs, under the bar), so it became a candidate for unseen-monster tests and nothing for the product. (8 Nov; Partly; source: docs/decisions.md#After shl-headline: @shl0.005 level on the headline; goal-1 bench tables read it beside @tsl1; running chains keep their leaf (on the owner's behalf, 2026-11-08))
- **Lane strength-by-hits-left.** Priced a monster's Strength by the hits it still has to land. It fixed one made-up mechanic on Gremlin Nob and made another much worse. (8 Nov; Didn't work; rated even beforehand; 3 CPU-hours)
- **Lane finish-faster-tiebreak.** Broke exact ties between options toward the faster win. In 330 headline fights it changed nothing. (8 Nov to 9 Nov; Didn't work; rated even beforehand; 1 CPU-hours)
- **Turn-start price in fights.** The owner split the turn-start price so it applies in played fights but not in the card chooser's trial fights, and on 11-13 that read level over 500 headline runs one turn deep (+0.56 end HP) with Demon Form picked on 0.19 of offers as before. (8 Nov to 13 Nov; Worked; source: docs/decisions.md#Level-defaults read: `@tsl1` and `@lcb1` pass, `@hpe` one turn deep fails; the one-turn controls move (on the owner's behalf, 2026-11-13))

#### Training the net

- **Burst nets and Demon Form.** Nets fine-tuned on eight made-up power cards, two of which grant Strength at turn start, already played the never-seen Demon Form better than the 10-29 net, by about 4 to 6 HP per cell-gauntlet run on decks holding it. (6 Nov; Worked; source: docs/decisions.md#Cards rung: nets that learned other turn-start powers already play Demon Form better; step 1c added (on the owner's behalf, 2026-11-06))
- **Lane crossed-player.** Let a fine-tuned net fight while the product's net picked the cards. About +1.6 HP per run one turn deep and +1.1 two turns deep, clears level: not enough to change the product. (6 Nov; Partly; rated likely beforehand; 12.6 CPU-hours)
- **Burst nets with turn-start price.** With turn-start powers priced in the search, the burst nets gained nothing more on Demon Form decks over cell-gauntlet runs, showing they had learned only Demon Form's first turn of payoff. (7 Nov; Didn't work; source: docs/decisions.md#After burst8 x turn-start leaf: what burst8 learned on Demon Form decks is the first tick; coverage-pretrain folds into the enemies bench; step-2 nets read against the turn-start leaf (on the owner's behalf, 2026-11-07))
- **Copy-rehearsal probe.** Mixing the original card back into short card bursts, to stop the net forgetting how to play it, made the forgetting worse at the elites (about 1.5 to 2.6 HP lost per elite fight instead of 0.7 to 1.4), so the probe stopped. (7 Nov; Didn't work; source: docs/decisions.md#Copy-rehearsal stopped: card bursts' elite cost is not missing rehearsal; no follow-up on card-burst retention (on the owner's behalf, 2026-11-07))
- **Lane all-content-net.** Trained a net from scratch on every content kind the engine had. It beat its twin on The Guardian and Hexaghost, mostly through Demon Form, and hit its CPU cap before the headline read. (7 Nov to 8 Nov; Partly; rated long shot beforehand; 31.6 CPU-hours)
- **One-mechanic family bursts.** A short burst of training on one Sharp Hide-like mechanic cut the cost of related made-up monster mechanics by more than half, for example Gremlin Nob that deals damage when attacked from about 13 to 2 HP per elite fight. (8 Nov; Worked; source: docs/decisions.md#After the family-transfer probes: the column keeps its per-mechanic families; a bench-wide retention probe queued; strength-by-hits-left and pack-pick-split carry ready charters; the review waits for the line (on the owner's behalf, 2026-11-08))
- **Bench-retention probes.** A 99-game burst on one made-up mechanic was played against the eight other generated mechanics and lost about 6 HP per fight on Sentries, so forgetting had to be read per monster, not averaged. (8 Nov; Partly; source: docs/decisions.md#After the bench-retention probes: a pack's net reads retention on the whole bench, per parent; a joint-burst probe queued (on the owner's behalf, 2026-11-08))
- **Joint burst over a pack.** One joint burst over all eight made-up mechanics kept the gain on Sentries but just missed its bar on Gremlin Nob and leaked about 3 HP per fight on Lagavulin. (8 Nov; Partly; source: docs/decisions.md#After the pack-joint-burst probe: a pack's net is one joint burst; pack-adapt-pipeline gets a ready charter (on the owner's behalf, 2026-11-08))
- **Training-variety ideas reviewed.** Of five owner ideas for varying training games, two were queued (forcing growth powers on the player, and an enemy that changes its moves) and the rest were parked. (8 Nov; Partly; the owner's call; source: docs/decisions.md#Curriculum modifiers reviewed: player growth exposure and Fickle queued, sleeping enemies wait for M2, run modifiers parked (owner, 2026-11-08))
- **Lane pack-retention.** Tried to stop that forgetting by anchoring the net to its parent. It fixed the old fights but traded one leak for another. (8 Nov; Didn't work; rated even beforehand; 7.5 CPU-hours)
- **Pack-dose at 33 games.** Training a pack of eight made-up mechanics at 33 games each with 90% replay of old games removed the Lagavulin leak but cut Gremlin Nob's cost by only a third and lost the Sentries gain. (9 Nov; Partly; source: docs/decisions.md#Candidate 44 ranked against the open lanes; pack-dose-33 read (on the owner's behalf, 2026-11-09))
- **Card bursts at high replay.** Card bursts of 33 games with 90% replay of old games cut the forgetting of the original card to about 0.5 HP per elite fight or less, but still closed nothing once turn-start powers were priced. (9 Nov; Partly; source: docs/decisions.md#After card-burst-share: between, no larger card dose (on the owner's behalf, 2026-11-09))
- **33-game quote widened.** Two made-up damage mechanics on Sentries were cut by about two thirds with only 33 training games at 90% replay, but one of them cost the net 1.7 HP per fight on the unmodified Sentries. (9 Nov; Partly; source: docs/decisions.md#After share-quote-widen: the 33-game quote widens on the cut, not on the parent (on the owner's behalf, 2026-11-09))
- **Lane shown-card-numbers.** Fed the net card numbers as the game shows them, with Strength and Weak already applied. Level without retraining. (9 Nov to 10 Nov; Didn't work; rated even beforehand; 2 CPU-hours)
- **Lane pack-net-scratch.** Trained three nets from scratch with the 31 new Act 1 cards. About +3.3 HP per run one turn deep on the new-card test, and the gain held fully two turns deep. One of them plays the real game today. (9 Nov to 10 Nov; Worked; rated even beforehand; 51.9 CPU-hours)

#### Run choices

- **Lane value-chooser.** Picked cards by the net's value of the new deck instead of trial fights. Level overall at a fraction of the time, but it misjudges cards the net never trained on. (6 Nov; Worked; rated even beforehand; 0.4 CPU-hours)
- **Lane pack-pick-split.** Tested whether real cards in the offers hurt because the AI picked them badly. Barring them lost even more, so the drop came from diluted offers. (8 Nov; Didn't work; rated even beforehand; 2.1 CPU-hours)
- **Headline changes deferred.** The owner decided the headline's small artificial card pool, and a rest-site rule that never upgrades held-out cards, will be fixed together once Act 1 is done so the controls are rebuilt only once. (9 Nov to 10 Nov; Dropped; the owner's call; source: docs/decisions.md#Headline card pool and choosers after Act 1 (owner, 2026-11-09))

#### Unseen content

- **Lane pattern-rows-burst.** Gave nets inputs for monster move patterns plus practice on Hexaghost. Wins barely moved. (6 Nov; Didn't work; rated long shot beforehand; 2.4 CPU-hours)
- **Lane act1-cards-zero-shot.** Played 22 real Act 1 cards the net had never seen. 19 were fine; Disarm, Impervious and Intimidate cost wins, mostly against elites, and Berserk helped. (7 Nov; Worked; rated likely beforehand; 1.6 CPU-hours)
- **Lane generated-enemies-bench.** Built a test of 24 made-up monster mechanics on official elites. 17 of 21 cost the net HP, mostly the ones that punish an action; it took two and a half times its CPU estimate. (7 Nov; Built; rated likely beforehand; 15.2 CPU-hours)
- **Lane gen-adapted-column.** Gave nets about 100 practice games on each made-up mechanic. Practice from the net's own games halved the cost on 4 of 4 mechanics. (7 Nov to 8 Nov; Worked; rated likely beforehand; 7.5 CPU-hours)
- **Lane few-game-recipe.** Taught a mechanic from only 33 games by mixing in old games. With the net's own games, four fifths of the cost went away. (8 Nov; Worked; rated even beforehand; 3.5 CPU-hours)
- **Lane share-curve.** Found how many old games to mix in when practising: with 90% old games, 33 new ones cut most of two mechanics' cost and kept old play. (8 Nov to 9 Nov; Worked; rated even beforehand; 9.85 CPU-hours)
- **Lane random-world-bench.** Stacked 2 to 8 made-up mechanics on one elite. The net's extra cost added up about as the single costs did, no worse. (9 Nov; Worked; rated even beforehand; 3.7 CPU-hours)

#### Tools and process

- **GitHub timeout not red.** One GitHub login timeout had turned the health light red and stopped every role, so a timeout now reads as unknown instead of as a blocker. (6 Nov; Worked; the owner's call; source: docs/decisions.md#Push blocker: a GitHub timeout is "can't tell", not red (owner, 2026-11-06))
- **Dashboard improvements.** The dashboard gained a zoomable 30-day timeline of jobs and sessions and kept its rule of being glanceable and read-only. (6 Nov; Built; the owner's call; source: docs/decisions.md#Dashboard improvements as built (owner, 2026-11-06))
- **Explorer v2.** The explorer was rebuilt to replay any test fight with the player that played it, show the board as the net sees it, and let the owner take over inside a fight. (7 Nov to 8 Nov; Built; the owner's call; source: docs/decisions.md#Explorer v2 as built (provisional, 2026-11-08))
- **Lane session-free-reads.** Let a family of 64 queued jobs run from one session to its verdict, with no worker checking in between. (7 Nov to 8 Nov; Worked; rated likely beforehand)
- **Element wording file.** Every content building block got its wording in one file, so the explorer writes all card, monster and power text from data and a test names any block without words. (8 Nov; Built; the owner's call; source: docs/decisions.md#Element wording (owner, spec docs/specs/element-wording.md, 2026-11-08))
- **Lane pack-adapt-pipeline.** Built the chain from a new content pack to a net that plays it, as queued jobs with no session in between. The nets it shipped still forgot a little old content. (8 Nov; Worked; rated likely beforehand; 3.8 CPU-hours)
- **Lane learn-memory.** Cut the memory of a 40,000-game training run to about 1 GB by streaming its game pool. (9 Nov; Worked; rated likely beforehand; 1.8 CPU-hours)
- **Lane queue-throughput.** Replayed the job queue to see whether smarter packing would use idle CPU. Almost none of the 19.8 idle hours could be recovered. (9 Nov; Didn't work; rated likely beforehand; 0.05 CPU-hours)

### Stretch 7: New nets for new content; relics and potions (10 Nov to 12 Nov)

43 things tried: Worked 13, Didn't work 11, Partly 5, Built 13, Still open 1.

#### Engine

- **Fight end as the game.** The recorded test games were changed to keep only what the real game keeps after a fight-ending blow, following the game's own action queue. (10 Nov; Built; the owner's call; source: docs/decisions.md#Golden games record an ended fight by what outlasts it (owner, 2026-11-10))
- **Hooks in written order.** Power hooks now apply their effects in written order using the stacks at that moment, so Poison takes HP and then drops a stack as the game does. (10 Nov; Built; the owner's call; source: docs/decisions.md#Hook effects scale by the stacks they see (owner, 2026-11-10))
- **Turn start before draw.** The start of a turn was split into a before-draw and an after-draw step in the game's order, which combat-start relics need. (10 Nov; Built; the owner's call; source: docs/decisions.md#Turn start before and after the draw, spec decisions (owner, 2026-11-10))
- **Card rules the net reads.** Rules such as Entangled's ban on attacks and Corruption's free exhausting Skills became inputs the net can learn instead of hidden special cases. (10 Nov; Built; the owner's call; source: docs/decisions.md#Card rules the net can read, spec decisions (owner, 2026-11-10))
- **Relics as their own thing.** Relics became their own kind of object on the player, each with its own triggers and counter, which the net can read as optional inputs. (11 Nov; Built; the owner's call; source: docs/decisions.md#Relics as their own entity, spec decisions (owner, 2026-11-11))
- **Fractional amounts.** Amounts like a share of max HP became fractions rounded the way the game's Java rounds them, which several relics and potions need. (11 Nov; Built; the owner's call; source: docs/decisions.md#Fractional amounts, spec decisions (owner, 2026-11-11))

#### Act 1 content

- **Game code decides rules.** The owner made the game's own decompiled Java code the final word on every number and behaviour, with the wiki and sts_lightspeed kept only as leads. (10 Nov; Worked; the owner's call; source: docs/decisions.md#Parity source: the game's own code (owner, 2026-11-10))
- **Block C6 card upgrades.** Card upgrades became data inside each card, including Searing Blow's repeated upgrades and Armaments. (10 Nov; Built; the owner's call; source: docs/decisions.md#Card upgrades as built (C6, owner-led session, 2026-11-10))
- **Relics R1 as content.** Relics arrived as content files, 23 at first, with Burning Blood's after-fight heal as a general amount other relics can raise. (10 Nov; Built; the owner's call; source: docs/decisions.md#Relics as content (R1), spec decisions (owner, 2026-11-10))
- **Red Skull as Strength.** Red Skull now gives real Strength when HP crosses half and takes it back on a heal, as the game does, so Heavy Blade and Limit Break count it. (11 Nov; Built; the owner's call; source: docs/decisions.md#Red Skull as real Strength, spec decisions (owner, 2026-11-10))
- **Potions in fights.** Potion slots and a use-potion action arrived with the first batch of Act 1 potions and Sacred Bark, reaching 25 of the 33 Act 1 potions by the end of 11-12. (11 Nov to 12 Nov; Built; the owner's call; source: docs/decisions.md#Potions, spec decisions (owner, 2026-11-11))
- **Relic batches of 11-12.** Relic batches added counter relics, the gamble relics and potions, after-block damage relics such as The Boot and Torii, Lizard Tail and Fairy in a Bottle, and card discovery, taking relics to 52 of 141 by the end of the day. (12 Nov; Built; the owner's call; source: docs/research/act1-relics-potions.md#Fight vocabulary (2026-11-12, owner session))

#### Search

- **Lane recurring-debuff-leaf.** Projected monsters' repeated Weak and Frail at the end of the search. It helped against Gremlin Nob and hurt against the Sentries. (10 Nov; Didn't work; rated even beforehand; 1.2 CPU-hours)
- **Twin depth paired by deck.** Paired deck by deck, the twin's gain from looking two turns ahead instead of one was +1.38 HP outside Demon Form decks over 500 runs, short of the bar, so one-turn screens stayed the rule. (11 Nov; Didn't work; source: docs/research/pns-probes.md#twin-depth-paired (0855-0857 with 0854): stop at 1.8 se; Demon Form splits by tick as predicted)
- **Lane counterfactual-state-price.** Priced each power by short playouts with and without it. Growth powers came out 10 to 25 times overpriced; closed at step 1. (11 Nov; Didn't work; rated long shot beforehand; 0.15 CPU-hours)

#### Training the net

- **Pack recipe at 99 games.** Training the pack of eight made-up mechanics at 99 games each with 90% replay closed the Lagavulin leak, cut most of Gremlin Nob's 13 HP cost per elite fight and kept the Sentries gain, so it became the pack recipe. (10 Nov; Worked; source: docs/decisions.md#After pack-dose 99s9: pack chains train 99 games per mechanic at share 0.9 (on the owner's behalf, 2026-11-10))
- **pns1-gen-bench probe.** The first net trained from scratch with the 31 new Ironclad cards, played on made-up monster mechanics it never saw, lost more HP than the 10-29 net on five of seven costly rows, so wider card training did not carry over to new monsters. (10 Nov; Didn't work; source: docs/research/pns-probes.md#pns1-gen-bench (0762): the pack net loses generated-bench play zero-shot)
- **pns-twin probe.** A twin net trained the same new way but without the new cards carried 87% of the pack net's gain on the new-card headline, so most of the gain came from the training recipe, and the new cards cost about 1.1 HP on official cell runs. (10 Nov; Partly; source: docs/research/pns-probes.md#pns-twin (0770-0773): the all-content recipe carries the pack net's gain; the pack costs official cells)
- **pns-twin-official probe.** On the plain headline one turn deep the twins gained only +0.76 HP over 500 runs, all of it on Demon Form decks, and lost 1.5 HP on the runs where nobody took Demon Form. (10 Nov; Didn't work; source: docs/research/pns-probes.md#pns-twin-official (0782-0783): the all-content gain on the ruler is Demon Form; outside it the twin loses 1.5 HP)
- **pns-twin-matched probe.** When the twins replayed the control's own card picks they fought level with the 10-29 net outside Demon Form decks, so their headline loss came from their card picks, not their fighting. (10 Nov; Partly; source: docs/research/pns-probes.md#pns-twin-matched (0791-0792): on the control's decks the twin fights level outside Demon Form)
- **twin-tsl1 probe.** With turn-start powers priced, the from-scratch twins still beat the 10-29 net by about 3.3 HP per cell-gauntlet run on Demon Form decks, the first sign a net had learned Demon Form's value beyond its first turn. (10 Nov; Worked; source: docs/research/pns-probes.md#twin-tsl1 (0793-0796): from-scratch games taught Demon Form more than its first tick)
- **pns-gen-attribution probe.** The twin without new cards also lost the made-up monster bench's gains, so the one-round 40k-game recipe itself, not the new cards, was weaker than the 10-29 net on mechanics neither had trained on. (10 Nov; Didn't work; source: docs/research/pns-probes.md#pns-gen-attribution, twin (0779): the recipe, not the pack, loses the generated bench)
- **pns-official probe.** The three new-card nets one turn deep on the plain headline gained only +0.78 HP over 500 runs, again all from Demon Form, so the product kept the 10-29 net. (10 Nov; Didn't work; source: docs/research/pns-probes.md#pns-official (0774-0776): the pack nets' ruler gain is Demon Form; outside it they lose 2.1 HP)
- **Lane player-growth-exposure.** Checked whether practising with growth powers teaches their later turns. The nets learned only the first tick of Strength; closed at step 1. (10 Nov; Didn't work; rated even beforehand; 1.2 CPU-hours)
- **pns-two-turn probes.** Played two turns deep on the new-card headline (sequences 0-249), the three new-card nets beat the product by +4.57 HP and about 0.10 clears pooled, as much as one turn deep, so gains on cards that pay off over many turns reach the product whole. (10 Nov to 11 Nov; Worked; source: docs/research/pns-probes.md#pns-two-turn-seeds (0819-0820): the gain carries whole two turns deep on three seeds)
- **Lane from-scratch-later-ticks.** Nets trained from scratch did learn Demon Form's later turns, and a 5,000-game fine-tune taught the old net about 2.7 HP per run on Demon Form decks. (10 Nov to 11 Nov; Worked; rated even beforehand; 10.6 CPU-hours)
- **Twin two turns deep.** The from-scratch twin two turns deep on the product's own decks gained +1.41 HP over 500 runs, about 1 HP outside Demon Form decks, a result between its go and stop bars. (11 Nov; Partly; source: docs/research/pns-probes.md#twin-two-turn-matched at 500 (0854): between; the fighter is worth about 1 HP outside Demon Form)
- **Later turns of new powers.** Ten nets that had learned from games what a power is worth on later turns all won +0.03 to +0.09 more fights on made-up recurring power cards no game held, closing the 10-29 net's gap to the hand-written player there. (11 Nov; Worked; source: docs/decisions.md#After the big-picture review of 2026-11-11: two charters amended, genpack-product-depth readied, 56 answered, pick book started (on the owner's behalf, 2026-11-11))
- **Lane luck-corrected-labels.** Tried to take draw luck out of the training targets. The correction added noise instead of removing it. (11 Nov; Didn't work; rated long shot beforehand; 0.6 CPU-hours)
- **Lane card-later-ticks-burst.** Fine-tuned on 5,000 games of Inflame and Metallicize starts. No better than 400 games at their later turns. (11 Nov; Didn't work; rated even beforehand; 5.3 CPU-hours)

#### Unseen content

- **Lane fickle-enemy.** Built a monster that punishes a different card type each turn. It cost the net too little to be worth teaching. (10 Nov; Didn't work; rated even beforehand; 3.8 CPU-hours)
- **Lane generated-pack-scratch.** Trained nets with 18 made-up monster mechanics. They removed three quarters of the cost of 6 mechanics they never saw, at half again the CPU estimate. (10 Nov to 11 Nov; Worked; rated even beforehand; 82.4 CPU-hours)
- **Stacked generated mechanics.** On official elites carrying four made-up mechanics at once, the net trained on generated monsters still removed about half the extra cost, so what it learned combines. (11 Nov; Worked; source: docs/research/genpack-reach-2026-11-11.md#Stacks (0842-0843): the cut composes, about half of the cost at the death cap)
- **Generated mechanics on bosses.** On the 325 official boss fights given made-up mechanics, the net trained on generated monsters removed about 0.8 of the cost of mechanics it never saw, though that cost was small at about 1.2 HP per fight. (11 Nov; Worked; source: docs/research/genpack-reach-2026-11-11.md#Bosses (0844-0845): the cut reaches the official bosses, on a small base cost)
- **Lane mechanic-count-curve.** Checked whether fewer made-up mechanics would do. 6 taught as well as 18 on one seed; a reboot cost half the lane's CPU. (11 Nov to 12 Nov; Worked; rated even beforehand; 37 CPU-hours)
- **Lane genpack-product-depth.** Played the made-up-mechanics net two turns deep. It removed between a quarter and a half of the cost: unclear, at nearly three times its CPU estimate. (11 Nov to 12 Nov; Partly; rated even beforehand; 32.8 CPU-hours)
- **Lane wider-generators.** Widened the made-up mechanics to a grid of 100. On one training seed the net removed most of the cost of unseen pairings, and half for a whole unseen trigger. (11 Nov to 13 Nov; Worked; rated even beforehand; 46.7 CPU-hours)
- **Wide net on owner's yardstick.** On the owner's yardstick of changed monsters (Sharp Hide 2, Thorns 2, Ritual 2, Weak on hit), the net trained on 100 made-up mechanics paid +0.92 HP of extra cost against +4.03 for the new-card net, with only Thorns 2 left. (12 Nov; Worked; source: docs/decisions.md#After genpack-yardstick: the wide pool moves the owner's yardstick; 70 joins 65, 67 retired (on the owner's behalf, 2026-11-12))
- **18-mechanic nets on yardstick.** The three nets trained on 18 made-up mechanics read +0.96 to +2.27 HP of extra cost on the same yardstick, so only one of three seeds reached half the new-card net's cost. (12 Nov; Partly; source: docs/decisions.md#After genpack18-yardstick: 65 re-chartered without 70; 70 a long shot (on the owner's behalf, 2026-11-12))
- **Lane genpack-burst-base.** Compared which net learns a new mechanic faster from 11 games. The one trained on made-up mechanics, by a wide margin. (12 Nov to 13 Nov; Worked; rated even beforehand; 2.5 CPU-hours)

#### Tests and rulers

- **Read floors for charters.** After the 11-10 review every lane had to show its read could detect the change it hoped for, using measured noise floors such as about 2.5 HP between nets over 500 headline runs. (10 Nov; Built; source: docs/decisions.md#After the big-picture review of 2026-11-10: read floors for charters; counterfactual pricing, product-depth worlds and wider generators ranked; 0780 cancelled; generated-pack-scratch's reroute written in advance (on the owner's behalf, 2026-11-10))

#### Tools and process

- **Lane machine-throughput (open).** Making the machine finish more work per hour. (12 Nov to 14 Nov; Still open)

### Stretch 8: The product changes; the real game (13 Nov to 14 Nov)

26 things tried: Worked 5, Didn't work 1, Built 18, Still open 2.

#### Engine

- **Engine audit.** An audit ranked 46 old patches in the engine against the game's code, found mismatches such as Artifact not blocking Flex's Strength loss, and the first fixes landed the same day. (13 Nov; Built; the owner's call; source: docs/research/engine-audit.md#Top five)
- **Card copies by value.** Cards in piles are now stored by value with wider card ids and no cap on changed copies, with search speed level, which let Snecko Eye and Snecko Oil load. (13 Nov; Built; the owner's call; source: docs/specs/sparse-piles.md)
- **Draw-pile rules.** Exact draw-pile rules were set (a card shuffled in at a random spot never lands on top), which changed the rules version, and a review of four designs for searching Purity's pick-any-number choice picked an exact method to build first. (13 Nov to 14 Nov; Built; the owner's call; source: docs/research/purity-search-designs-2026-11-13.md)
- **Rule fixes found live.** The real game showed rule gaps that were fixed the same morning: The Guardian keeping its 20 block when Mode Shift breaks at turn end, Perfected Strike counting itself, turn-end relics like Orichalcum acting first, and Feed's max HP on a kill. (14 Nov; Built; the owner's call; source: 626955c6)

#### Act 1 content

- **Game jar as rules source.** Once the owner installed the game, its own jar became the first place to look up a rule, and the real game became the top test arena. (13 Nov; Built; source: docs/decisions.md#Parity source and arena after the install: the game's jar first, the real game is arena level 2 (on the owner's behalf, 2026-11-13))
- **Single-entry Act 1 blocks.** A batch of one-off pieces added Havoc, Champion Belt, Smoke Bomb's escape, Entropic Brew and Potion Belt. (13 Nov; Built; the owner's call; source: docs/specs/act1-singles.md)
- **Colorless cards.** Twenty-five colorless cards, Warcry, the Colorless Potion and Toolbox were added. (13 Nov; Built; the owner's call; source: docs/specs/colorless-cards.md)
- **Blocked relics from the jar.** Readers checked each relic the engine could not yet express against the game's jar, and in seven steps relics such as Chemical X, Ice Cream, Runic Pyramid, Paper Phrog and Neow's Lament were added, bringing relics to 76 of 141. (13 Nov to 14 Nov; Built; the owner's call; source: docs/research/blocked-relics-2026-11-13.md)
- **Last four Ironclad cards.** A spec for the last four Ironclad cards was written and Battle Trance and Reaper were built, leaving Dual Wield and Juggernaut. (14 Nov; Built; the owner's call; source: docs/specs/ironclad-last-four.md)

#### Search

- **End-HP bonus scales.** The search's bonus for HP left became a share of max HP, so a win is worth the same at any max HP, which changed nothing at the 80 max HP of every fight so far. (13 Nov; Built; the owner's call; source: docs/decisions.md#End-HP bonus scales with max HP (owner, 2026-11-13))
- **Leftover block price.** Pricing block left at the end of the turn read level over 500 headline runs one turn deep (0.700 against 0.680 clears) and cut deaths at the second elite from 31 to 24, so it passed under the new rule. (13 Nov; Worked; source: docs/decisions.md#Level-defaults read: `@tsl1` and `@lcb1` pass, `@hpe` one turn deep fails; the one-turn controls move (on the owner's behalf, 2026-11-13))
- **End-HP bonus one turn.** Using the product's end-HP bonus in one-turn play lost 1.74 HP over 500 headline runs, because it treats HP within the after-fight heal as free while the fight is still going, and the flaw was logged for a fix. (13 Nov; Didn't work; source: docs/decisions.md#Level-defaults read: `@tsl1` and `@lcb1` pass, `@hpe` one turn deep fails; the one-turn controls move (on the owner's behalf, 2026-11-13))
- **Product gains both prices.** The two-turn product with both new prices and the matching card chooser read level with the old product (0.832 against 0.834 clears over 500 headline runs), and became the first product change since 11-03. (13 Nov; Worked; source: docs/decisions.md#Product read 0986: `@tsl1@lcb1` two turns deep is level; the product gains both flags and the chooser (on the owner's behalf, 2026-11-13))

#### Training the net
