- **Lane blind-row-kinds (open).** Making sure every building block a custom power or monster can use shows up in the net's inputs; some, like end-of-round hooks, were invisible to it. (13 Nov to 14 Nov; Still open)

#### Run choices

- **Real-game bridge.** A bridge was built that reads the real game through CommunicationMod, rebuilds each state as an engine fight and plays it with the product, tested first against a fake game. (13 Nov; Built; the owner's call; source: docs/specs/real-game-bridge.md)
- **Whole runs in real game.** The controller took over whole real runs, from Neow and the map to card rewards, rest sites and shops, with a simple rule player finishing any fight the engine cannot model. (14 Nov; Built; the owner's call; source: 93cd5661)
- **Real Act 1 runs.** The AI played whole real Ascension 0 Ironclad runs all day and beat the Act 1 boss in most of them, while the engine predicted the game's next state right on almost every checked step; the real-game section keeps the live count. (14 Nov; Worked; the owner's call; source: docs/research/real-game-runs.md#Summary)
- **Bridge fixes from live runs.** Live runs exposed bridge gaps that were fixed between runs, such as free cards made in a fight, powers the game shows without a number, and Blood for Blood's cost from HP lost. (14 Nov; Built; the owner's call; source: docs/research/real-game-runs.md)
- **Ascension plan.** The bridge gained an ascension setting, and a plan for ascension levels up to 20 inside fights was written from the game's jar but not yet built. (14 Nov; Built; the owner's call; source: docs/specs/ascension.md)

#### Unseen content

- **Lane wide-recipe-confirm (open).** Two more training seeds for the 100-mechanic recipe. (13 Nov to 14 Nov; Still open)

#### Tests and rulers

- **Level is a pass.** The owner ruled that a change that is more correct or more general passes when it reads level or better, instead of needing a +1.5 HP gain, and four earlier fails were read again. (13 Nov; Worked; the owner's call; source: docs/decisions.md#Level and more general is a pass (owner, 2026-11-13))
- **New one-turn control.** The turn-start and leftover-block prices together read 0.716 against 0.680 clears over 500 headline runs one turn deep, so they became the new one-turn yardstick. (13 Nov; Worked; source: docs/decisions.md#Combined one-turn control `@tsl1@lcb1` read; the controls move to it; the product read with both flags queued (on the owner's behalf, 2026-11-13))

#### Tools and process

- **Lane files and daily curate.** Lane charters moved into one file per lane and a daily task was added to keep the stored facts short and current. (13 Nov; Built; the owner's call; source: 7910ce0d)
- **Docs index and hook.** A script now writes the docs index with each spec's status and the Act 1 coverage count, and a commit hook refuses an index that is out of date. (13 Nov; Built; the owner's call; source: 81aa83b9)
- **Showcase site.** A hand-written showcase site for Slay the Spire players was published, with each real run added as it finished. (14 Nov; Built; the owner's call; source: 29c00ea3)

Reading the numbers

## A short glossary

**Clears** The share of runs that beat the Act 1 boss. 0.834 means 417 of 500.

**End HP** HP left after the boss, 0 for a run that died. Used for paired comparisons.

**se** Standard error, the noise range of a number. A difference under about 2 se may be luck.

**One turn / two turns deep** How far the search looks: to the end of this turn, or through the next one with 4 sampled hands.

**Runs 0–499 / 500–999** Two fixed books of seeded runs. Settings were tuned on the first; the second checks them.

**The product** The best player the project makes: the 29 October net, two turns deep, with the HP price and line step.

**Hand-written player** A formula that scores positions from card and intent numbers. The baseline every net is compared with.

**Goal 1 and goal 2** Goal 1: play well on content the AI has never seen. Goal 2: be as strong at Slay the Spire as Stockfish is at chess.

Written by hand on 14 November 2026 from the project's own records: `git log`, `docs/decisions.md`, `docs/digest/`, `docs/research/`, the fact store in `docs/claims/`, and result files under `results/`. Every number was checked against its file. The chart data comes from `showcase/extract.py`, which reads finished result files and nothing else. A plain-text edition of this page for language models, with every chart as a table: llms.md (the page) and llms-full.md (adds every real-game fight, the full list of everything tried and the statistics data).
